**Points that change how the values should be used**

\- **Reference standard drives the upper limb tension test discrepancy.** The pooled MRI-referenced estimate gives a much weaker rule-out value than LR− 0.12. A negative result on all four neurodynamic tests performs better, with pooled LR− 0.06 (0.02–0.25). The crosshair plot from the 2026 Thoomes meta-analysis shows these pooled estimates.[<u>\[1\]</u>](https://doi.org/10.1186/s12891-026-09551-0)  
  
<img src="media/image4.jpeg" style="width:10.09236in;height:10.49167in" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/openevidence-multimedia/springer/cms/10.1186_s12891-026-09551-0/asset/0c17657c-0a10-5206-86a9-0e501fe64bfd/assets/images/large/12891_2026_9551_Fig3_HTML.jpg" />Figure 3 Crosshair plot of diagnostic test accuracy meta-analysis. The plot shows pooled sensitivity (y-axis) against false positive rate (1 − specificity, x-axis) for each index test. Individual study results appear as smaller dots in the same colour as their corresponding test. Large solid symbols (triangle = combined 4 ULNTs, diamond = shoulder abduction test, square = ULNT1) indicate pooled estimates from the bivariate GLMM. Vertical and horizontal bars represent 95% confidence intervals for sensitivity and false positive rate. Colours correspond to test type: red = combined 4 ULNTs, green = shoulder abduction test, blue = ULNT1. ULNT: upper limb neurodynamic test  
  
- **The myelopathy values overstate specificity for neck-pain patients.** Controls were healthy volunteers, and the case definition required the same symptoms being tested. A 2025 scoping review of case-control studies gives similar figures: hand incoordination Se 52%, Sp 92%; gait imbalance Se 56–63%, Sp 52–95%. The Soufi figure below shows the symptom-level sensitivity and specificity behind P8–P10.<u>[\[9\]](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-025-20928-4)[\[12\]](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40011743)</u>  
  
<img src="media/image21.jpeg" style="width:15.6125in;height:7.70486in" alt="https://storage.googleapis.com/openevidence-multimedia/springer/cms/10.1038_s41598-025-20928-4/asset/a24538b2-b2d8-55da-a3c4-7bd122a5a77b/assets/images/large/41598_2025_20928_Fig1_HTML.jpg" />Figure 1 Diagnostic accuracy of symptoms between healthy controls (HCS) and DCM patients. The plots are based on the data reported in the results section. DCM with n=139; healthy with n=108. Neck Pain (SE=85%, SP=78%, YI=63%); UE numbness (SE=67%, SP=90%, YI=57%); hand incoordination (SE=58%, SP=92%, YI=50%); gait imbalance (SE=58%, SP=92%, YI=50%); UE weakness (SE=53%, SP=93%, YI =46%); arm pain (SE=51%, SP=90%, YI=41%); back pain (SE=72%, SP=67%, YI=40%); headache SE=58%, SP77%, YI=36%); lower extremity weakness (SE=39%, SP=95%, YI=34%); urinary dysfunction (SE= 41%, SP=91%, YI=32%) lower extremity numbness (SE=38%, SP=91%, YI=29%); leg pain (SE=40%, SP=84%, YI=24%);fecal incontinence (SE=12%, SP=98%, YI=10%); sexual dysfunction (SE=14%, SP=94%, YI=9%); SE=58%, SP, YI=%); saddle numbness (SE=6%, SP=98%, YI=4%). ‘\*’ was used to denote a trend (uncorrected p \< 0.05), ‘\*\*’ represents statistical significance (corrected p \< 0.05), and ‘\*\*\*’ indicates a highly significant result (corrected p \< 0.001).  
  
- **Thoracic outlet history items cannot be given likelihood ratios.** Both are built into the clinical criteria, and the cohorts report no frequencies for patients who did not meet the criteria. Systematic reviews found little evidence supporting the validity of the examination maneuvers either.<u>[\[10\]](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28735950)[\[11\]](https://doi.org/10.1007/s12178-020-09643-x)[\[13\]](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30871714)</u>  
  
- **Cells needing full-text confirmation:** Wainner 2003 results tables (all ‡ values and their confidence intervals) and the Sleijser-Koehorst 2021 item-level table for P1.  
  
Would it be useful to look at which combinations of myelopathy signs (Hoffmann, Trömner, inverted brachioradialis, hyperreflexia) have accuracy data against symptomatic neck-pain controls rather than healthy volunteers?
